L793: ## 1 Executive Summary
L794: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L795: Novartis submitted NDA 207997 for midostaurin (Rydapt®), also known as PKC412 or CGP41251, a new molecular entity (NME) for use in combination with chemotherapy for the treatment of previously untreated acute myeloid leukemia (AML) with *fms*-like tyrosine kinase-3 (FLT3) kinase domain mutations or internal tandem duplications. Midostaurin inhibits oncogenic mutant and wild-type forms of FLT3, KIT kinase, vascular endothelial growth factor receptor 2 (VEGFR2), fibroblast growth factor receptor (FGFR), members of the serine/threonine kinase family protein kinase C (PKC) and other receptor tyrosine kinases.
L796: The applicant's proposed indication is "in combination with standard induction and consolidation chemotherapy [REDACTED] (b) (4) for adult patients with newly diagnosed AML who are FLT3 mutation-positive, as detected by an FDA approved test". The proposed dosing schedule for patients with acute myeloid leukemia is 50 mg administered orally twice daily on days 8-21 of each cycle of induction and consolidation chemotherapy, [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L797: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L798: I conclude that the information submitted by the applicant has provided substantial evidence for the effectiveness of midostaurin, in combination with standard cytarabine and daunorubicin induction and cytarabine consolidation chemotherapy, for the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed AML who are FLT3 mutation-positive, as detected by an FDA approved test, and supports full approval in this indication. This conclusion is based on the results of a single randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study in previously untreated patients age \le 60 years that demonstrated an overall survival advantage in patients who received midostaurin in addition to standard chemotherapy compared with those who did not (HR 0.77, P=0.016). The study included a maintenance phase in which patients continued either midostaurin or placebo as a single agent after completion of standard chemotherapy. However, the study was not re-randomized at the start of maintenance, and as a result, the applicant provided insufficient evidence to support the effectiveness of midostaurin maintenance. The applicant provided a summary of safety and efficacy data from an investigator-sponsored trial of midostaurin in combination with intensive chemotherapy for untreated AML that included adults age &gt; 60, supporting an indication unrestricted by age. The applicant provided data from several studies of midostaurin monotherapy in patients with previously untreated and relapsed/refractory AML that showed no evidence of clinical benefit of monotherapy in these indications, supporting a limitation of use.
L799: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L800: ## Clinical Review
L801: Ashley Ward, MD
L802: NDA 207997
L803: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L804: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L805: Midostaurin is a small molecule that inhibits multiple receptor tyrosine kinases. In vitro biochemical or cellular assays have shown that midostaurin or its major human active metabolites CGP62221 and CGP52421 inhibits the activity of FMS-like tyrosine kinase 3 FLT3 (wild type and D835Y mutant) and many other kinases. Approximately 35% of patients with AML harbor mutations of the *FLT3* gene on chromosome 13q12 which result in constitutive activation of FLT3 kinase. These mutations can be internal tandem duplications (ITD) or point mutations in the tyrosine kinase domain (TKD). FLT3-ITD mutations are associated with an increased likelihood of relapse and decreased overall survival (OS) in AML, while the prognostic effect of FLT3-TKD mutations is still under debate. The applicant's proposed indication evaluated in this review is "in combination with standard induction and consolidation chemotherapy (b) (4) for adult patients with newly diagnosed AML who are FLT3 mutation-positive, as detected by an FDA approved test". The benefit-risk assessment supports full approval of midostaurin, in combination with standard cytarabine and daunorubicin induction and cytarabine consolidation for the treatment of adults with FLT3+ AML. (b) (4)
L806: The efficacy of midostaurin is primarily based on the results of a single large randomized, double-blinded, placebo-controlled phase 3 study that compared midostaurin, administered at 50 mg by mouth twice daily on days 8-21 of each cycle of induction and consolidation chemotherapy, followed by continuous daily therapy at the same dose for up to 12 months of maintenance, to placebo in patients with previously untreated FLT3+ AML. The study was jointly sponsored by a cooperative group (CALGB, now Alliance) and the commercial sponsor. A total of 717 patients (360 on the midostaurin arm and 357 on the placebo arm) were evaluated on this study, which found that midostaurin yielded a statistically significant OS advantage (HR=0.77, p=0.016). Due to plateauing of the OS curves, estimates of median OS are not reliable, but the restricted mean survival advantage ranges from 2.6 - 5.4 months depending on time of follow-up. The median event-free survival (EFS) was 8.2 months on the midostaurin arm and 3.6 months on the placebo arm. Rates of complete response (CR) by day 60 were also higher on the midostaurin arm (59% vs 54%). Although the pivotal study was conducted in patients age &lt; 60, data from an investigator-sponsored trial of midostaurin in combination with intensive chemotherapy in older adults with AML lend support for a broad indication without age restriction. Data from several studies of midostaurin monotherapy in patients with newly-diagnosed or relapsed/refractory AML show toxicity without substantial clinical activity, and resulted in a limitation of use statement in the product label.
L807: The safety database for midostaurin consists of 1,298 patients exposed to midostaurin across 24 studies in patients with AML, patients with other malignancies, patients without malignancy and healthy volunteers. In general, midostaurin appears well-tolerated. Most fatal adverse
L808: ## Clinical Review
L809: Ashley Ward, MD
L810: NDA 207997
L811: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L812: events (AEs) occurred on studies of patients with AML receiving intensive chemotherapy, and were less frequent in the midostaurin arm than the placebo arm on the pivotal study. These events primarily consisted of infections, which are common causes of treatment-related mortality in this patient population. The most frequent serious adverse reaction was febrile neutropenia. Common adverse reactions (&gt;20%) included anemia, nausea, rash, vomiting, mucositis, headache, elevated transaminases, musculoskeletal pain, petechiae, hyperglycemia, device-related infections and epistaxis. The high frequency of mild-moderate nausea and vomiting observed with midostaurin use warrants a recommendation to administer concomitant anti-emetics. As nonclinical reproductive studies noted fetal anomalies and fetal loss secondary to midostaurin, and the chemotherapy regimen midostaurin will be indicated for use with is frequently used in pregnant women with AML, I recommend a boxed warning against use in pregnancy.

Analysis of Condition	There were 19,950 new cases of AML and 10,430 deaths from AML in the United States in 2016. AML occurs in all age groups, but is primarily a disease of older adults, with a median age at diagnosis of 67 years.

AML is a fatal disease.

Older patients (age ≥ 60) fare less well, with CR rates of approximately 50% and 2-year overall survival of approximately 20%.
Current standard of care regimens are insufficient, and more effective therapies are needed.
Benefit	Study A2301 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study of midostaurin in combination with up to 6 cycles of intensive chemotherapy, followed by 12 months of maintenance with monotherapy, in patients with previously untreated FLT3+ AML.
The midostaurin arm had a statistically significant improvement in overall survival (HR 0.77, p=0.016).
Midostaurin 50 mg bid on days 8-21 in combination with daunorubicin / cytarabine for up to 2 cycles of induction and high dose cytarabine for up to 4 cycles of consolidation improves survival in patients with FLT3+ AML compared to standard therapy.

L820: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L821: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L822: Acute myeloid leukemia (AML) is a heterogeneous group of hematopoietic neoplasms characterized by a clonal proliferation of myeloid precursors with limited ability to differentiate into more mature myeloid cells. These blasts replace normal hematopoietic tissue in the bone marrow, resulting in pancytopenia. According to the National Cancer Institute's SEER database, it is estimated that there were 19,950 new cases of AML and 10,430 deaths from AML in the United States in 2016. AML occurs in children and adults of all ages, but is primarily a disease of older adults, with a median age at diagnosis of 67 years. AML is more common in men than women (5.0 vs 3.4 new cases per 100,000 persons per year) and does not have a strong racial or ethnic predilection. AML is universally fatal without treatment, with a median survival of approximately two months (Oran and Weisdorf, Haematologica 2012).
L823: Approximately 35% of patients with AML harbor mutations of the FMS-like tyrosine kinase 3 (*FLT3*) gene on chromosome 13q12. These mutations typically result in either an internal tandem duplication (ITD) of amino acids in the juxtamembrane region of the *FLT3* protein or, less frequently, a point mutation in the activation loop of the tyrosine kinase domain (TKD). Both ITD and TKD mutations result in constitutive activation of *FLT3* kinase. *FLT3*-ITD mutations of any length are associated with an increased likelihood of relapse (Schnittger et al, Blood 2002), and *FLT3*-ITD mutations with a high mutant/wild type ratio are associated with decreased overall survival (Thiede et al, Blood 2002). The prognostic effect of *FLT3*-TKD mutations is still under debate, and may be dependent on additional mutations in individual patients (Bacher et al, Blood 2008).
L824: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L825: With the exception of a rare subtype of AML called acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL), which is frequently curable with retinoic acid and arsenic trioxide and will be excluded from further discussion in this review, combination chemotherapy regimens with or without hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT) are a mainstay of therapy for patients with AML. A list of chemotherapy agents with FDA approval for the treatment of AML is provided in Table 1. In patients who can tolerate intensive therapy, which may be limited by factors such as age and comorbid conditions, cytarabine and daunorubicin induction followed by high-dose cytarabine consolidation (the so-called "7+3" regimen) is frequently used. This regimen typically results in CR rates of 60-70% and 2-year OS of approximately 50% in patients &lt; 60 years of age (Fernandez et al, NEJM 2009). However, older patients (age ≥ 60) fare less well, with CR rates of approximately 50% and 2-year overall survival of approximately 20% (Estey, JCO 2006). While
L826: ## Clinical Review
L827: Ashley Ward, MD
L828: NDA 207997
L829: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L830: Improved HSCT and supportive care regimens have reduced early death rates, no new therapies that confer a survival advantage in AML have been developed since the “7+3” regimen was shown to be effective in the early 1970s (Yates et al, Cancer Chemotherapy Reports 1973). There remains a clear need for new treatments for patients with AML.
L831: **Table 1: Currently Available Treatments for Acute Myelogenous Leukemia**
L832: Agent	Excerpted Indication
Cyclophosphamide	Indicated for the treatment of acute myelogenous and monocytic leukemia. Although effective alone in susceptible malignancies, is more frequently used concurrently or sequentially with other antineoplastic drugs.
Cytarabine	Indicated, in combination with other approved anticancer drugs, for remission induction in acute non-lymphocytic leukemia of adults and children.
Daunorubicin	Indicated, in combination with other approved anticancer drugs, for remission induction in acute non-lymphocytic leukemia of adults.
Doxorubicin	Has been used successfully to produce regression in disseminated neoplastic conditions including acute myeloblastic leukemia.
Idarubicin	Indicated, in combination with other approved anti-leukemic drugs, for the treatment of acute myeloid leukemia in adults. This includes FAB classifications M1 through M7.
Mitoxantrone	Indicated, in combination with other approved drugs, in the initial therapy of acute nonlymphocytic leukemia in adults. This category includes myelogenous, promyelocytic, monocytic and erythroid acute leukemias.
Thioguanine	Indicated for remission induction and remission consolidation treatment of acute nonlymphocytic leukemias. Is not recommended for use during maintenance therapy or similar long-term continuous treatments due to the high risk of liver toxicity. Reliance upon thioguanine alone is seldom justified for initial remission induction of acute non-lymphocytic leukemias because combination chemotherapy including thioguanine results in more frequent remission induction and longer duration of remission than thioguanine alone.
Vincristine	Indicated in acute leukemia.

L837: ## 3.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L838: ## Clinical Review
L839: Ashley Ward, MD
L840: NDA 207997
L841: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L842: The trials included in this application were conducted under IND 057120, which was opened in the United States in November of 1998. The IND has never been placed on clinical hold.
L843: The FDA granted orphan designation (#09-2859) to midostaurin for the treatment of acute myeloid leukemia on July 7, 2009.
L844: At the end of phase 2 meeting held on July 12, 2006, the FDA generally agreed with dose, regimen, and patient population proposed for Study A2301. However, there was disagreement about [REDACTED] (b) (4) and the Agency encouraged the Sponsor to submit the study for Special Protocol Assessment (SPA). A request for SPA was received by the FDA on January 12, 2007. After this more detailed review of the protocol, there was continued disagreement between the FDA and the Sponsor about [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L845: As a result of these and other potential deficiencies, the FDA determined that the design and planned analysis of the study did not address the objectives necessary to support a regulatory submission and there was no SPA agreement.
L846: The FDA granted breakthrough therapy designation for the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed AML who are FLT3 mutation positive as detected by an FDA-approved test and who are eligible to receive standard induction and consolidation chemotherapy on January 29, 2016.
L847: This NDA was submitted under a rolling submission, for which it qualified after receiving breakthrough therapy designation. Part 1 included the complete nonclinical module, and was submitted April 5, 2016. Part 2 included the complete quality module, and was submitted May 26, 2016. Part 3 included the summary of clinical efficacy, and was submitted July 25, 2016. Part 4 (the final part) included the clinical module and the summary of clinical safety, and was submitted August 29, 2016. The Applicant requested priority review with the submission of the final portion of the NDA, and the FDA granted priority review on October 28, 2016.

L860: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L861: Midostaurin is supplied as a soft capsule containing 25 mg of midostaurin. It is intended for oral administration. The capsule contains polyoxyl 40 hydrogenated castor oil, gelatin, polyethylene glycol 400, glycerin 85%, dehydrated alcohol, corn oil mono-di-triglycerides, titanium dioxide, vitamin E, ferric oxide yellow, ferric oxide red, carmine, hypromellose 2910, propylene glycol, and purified water. The molecular formula for midostaurin is C_{35}H_{30}N_4O_4. The molecular weight is 570.65 g/mole. The chemical structure of midostaurin is shown below:
L862: No product quality concerns were identified on preliminary CMC review, and approval is recommended.
L863: ## 4.3. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L864: In mouse, rats and dogs, PKC412 administered orally caused end-organ toxicities in multiple organ/systems. The organs/systems involved were gastrointestinal tract (gastric hemorrhage,
L865: ## Clinical Review
L866: Ashley Ward, MD
L867: NDA 207997
L868: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L869: diarrhea, emesis and loose feces), liver (↑ organ weight, ↑AST, ALT), immune system (thymic atrophy, lymphoid depletion in lymph nodes), hematopoietic system (low red blood cell parameters, bone marrow hypocellularity and hemorrhage), lungs (bronchopneumonia, alveolar hemorrhage), heart (prolongation in P-Q interval), reproductive system (reduced spermatogenesis and organ weight of prostate and seminal vesicle, uterine atrophy, and follicular distension of ovaries), and possibly the neuromuscular system (ataxia, abnormal gait).
L870: Carcinogenicity studies have not been performed with midostaurin. Midostaurin was not mutagenic in vitro in the bacterial reverse mutation assay (Ames test) or in Chinese hamster V97 cells. Midostaurin increased the frequency of polyploidy cells in an in vitro chromosomal aberrations assay in Chinese hamster ovary cells, but was not clastogenic in an in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus assay when tested to the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) of 200 mg/kg (1200 mg/m2). This dose was approximately 20-fold the recommended human dose, based on body surface area.
L871: Based on nonclinical findings, male and female fertility may be compromised by treatment with midostaurin. Reproductive toxicity was observed in a fertility study, in which treated female rats were mated with treated males at oral doses of 10, 30 and 60 mg/kg/day of midostaurin (approximately 0.02, 0.11, and 0.13 times, respectively, the AUC in patients with cancer at the recommended human dose of 50 mg BID). In males, testicular degeneration and atrophy was observed at doses greater than or equal to 10 mg/kg/day and reduced and reduced sperm count and motility, and a decrease in reproductive organ weights were observed at 60 mg/kg/day. In females, increased resorptions (including total resorption of litter), decreased pregnancy rate, number of implants and live embryos were observed at 60 mg/kg/day. When F1 generation males were mated with F1 females from dams treated with 30 mg/kg/day midostaurin in an oral pre- and postnatal development study in the rat, the number of implantation sites and live fetuses/litter were reduced. In a 3-month toxicology study in dogs, there was inhibition of spermatogenesis was seen in dogs at doses greater than or equal to 3 mg/kg/day (approximately 0.012 times the exposure measured by AUC in patients with cancer at the recommended human dose).
L872: The preclinical pharmacology/toxicology reviewer recommended approval.
L873: ## 4.4. Clinical Pharmacology
L874: Clinical pharmacology reviewers determined that the evidence of effectiveness and the proposed dose and regimen were sufficiently supported by the application and recommended approval.
L875: ## 4.4.1. Mechanism of Action
L876: Midostaurin is a small molecule that inhibits multiple receptor tyrosine kinases. In vitro
L877: ## Clinical Review
L878: Ashley Ward, MD
L879: NDA 207997
L880: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L881: biochemical or cellular assays have shown that midostaurin or its major human active metabolites CGP62221 and CGP52421 inhibit the activity of FLT3 (wild type and D835Y mutant), KIT D816V mutant, SYK, CSF-1R, PDGFR\alpha, PDGFR\beta, and ROS1, as well as members of the serine/threonine kinase PKC family. In addition, midostaurin inhibits several other receptor tyrosine kinases like FGFR and VEGFR2.
L882: Midostaurin demonstrated the ability to inhibit FLT3 receptor signaling and cell proliferation and induce apoptosis in leukemic cells expressing ITD and TKD mutant FLT3 receptors or overexpressing wild type FLT3 and PDGF receptors. Midostaurin also demonstrated the ability to inhibit KIT signaling, cell proliferation and histamine release, and induce apoptosis in mast cells.
L883: ## 4.4.2. Pharmacokinetics
L884: Midostaurin exhibits time-dependent pharmacokinetics with an initial increase in trough concentrations (C_{min}) that reach maximal concentrations during the first week followed by a decline to a steady-state after approximately 28 days. The pharmacokinetics of the CGP62221 showed a similar trend. The plasma concentrations of CGP52421 continued to increase after one month of treatment. The maximal trough concentrations and steady-state concentrations of midostaurin, CGP62221, and CGP52421 were similar when RYDAPT was administered at a dose of 50 mg twice daily or 100 mg twice daily with food.
L885: **Absorption:** The time to maximal concentrations (T_{max}) occurred between 1 to 3 hours post dose in the fasted state. Midostaurin exposure, represented by area under the curve over time to infinity (AUC_{inf}), increased 1.2-fold when midostaurin was co-administered with a standard meal (457 calories, 50 g fat, 21 g proteins, and 18 g carbohydrates) and 1.6-fold when co-administered with a high-fat meal (1007 calories, 66 g fat, 32 g proteins, and 64 g carbohydrates) compared to when midostaurin was administered in a fasted state. Midostaurin maximum concentrations (C_{max}) were reduced by 20% with a standard meal and by 27% with a high-fat meal compared to a fasted state. T_{max} was delayed when midostaurin was administered with a standard meal or a high-fat meal (median T_{max} = 2.5 hrs to 3 hrs).
L886: **Distribution:** Midostaurin has an estimated mean volume of distribution of 95.2 L. Midostaurin and its metabolites are distributed mainly in plasma in vitro. Midostaurin, CGP62221, and CGP52421 are greater than 99.8% bound to plasma protein in vitro. Midostaurin is mainly bound to \alpha1-acid glycoprotein in vitro.
L887: **Elimination:** The geometric mean terminal half-life (% coefficient of variation) is 21 hours (19%) for midostaurin, 32 hours (31%) for CGP62221 and 482 hours (25%) for CGP52421. Midostaurin is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4. CGP62221 and CGP52421 account for 28 \pm 2.7\% and 39 \pm 6.6\% (mean \pm standard deviation) respectively of the total circulating radioactivity. Fecal excretion accounted for 95% of the recovered dose with 91% of the recovered dose excreted as
L888: ## Clinical Review
L889: Ashley Ward, MD
L890: NDA 207997
L891: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L892: metabolites and 4% of the recovered dose as unchanged midostaurin. Only 5% of the recovered dose was found in urine.
L893: **Specific Populations:** Age (20-94 years), sex, race, and mild (total bilirubin &gt; 1.0 to 1.5 times the upper limit of normal (ULN) or aspartate aminotransferase (AST) greater than the ULN) or moderate (total bilirubin 1.5 to 3.0 times the ULN and any value for AST) hepatic impairment or renal impairment (creatinine clearance (CLcr) &gt; 30 mL/min) did not have clinically meaningful effects on the pharmacokinetics of midostaurin, CGP62221, or CGP52421. The pharmacokinetics of midostaurin in patients with baseline severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin greater than 3.0 times the ULN and any value for AST) or severe renal impairment (CLcr 15 to 29 mL/min) is unknown.
L894: **Effect of Strong CYP3A4 Inhibitors on Midostaurin:** Coadministration of ketoconazole (400 mg daily for 10 days) with a single dose of midostaurin (50 mg) on Day 6 increased the AUC_{inf} of midostaurin by 10.4-fold and CGP62221 by 3.5-fold and area under the curve over time to last measurable concentrations (AUC_{0-t}) of CGP52421 by 1.2-fold compared to a single RYDAPT dose coadministered with placebo. Coadministration of itraconazole (100 mg twice daily on Days 22-28 for 13 doses) with multiple doses of midostaurin (100 mg twice daily on Days 1 to 2 and 50 mg twice daily on Days 3 to 28) increased Day 28 trough concentrations of midostaurin by 2.1-fold, CGP62221 by 1.2-fold, and CGP52421 by 1.3-fold compared to the respective Day 21 trough concentrations with midostaurin alone.
L895: **Effect of Strong CYP3A4 Inducers on Midostaurin:** Coadministration of rifampicin (600 mg daily on Days 1 to 14) with a single dose of midostaurin (50 mg) on Day 9 decreased the AUC_{inf} of midostaurin by 96%, the AUC_{inf} of CGP62221 by 92%, and the AUC_{0-t} of CGP52421 by 59%.
L896: **Effect of Midostaurin on Sensitive CYP3A substrates:** Midazolam (sensitive CYP3A substrate) AUC_{inf} was not affected following 4 days of midostaurin administration. The clinical relevance of this interaction is unknown as the midostaurin was administered for only 4 days.
L897: **Effects of Midostaurin:** Midostaurin, CGP52421 and CGP62221 inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP2E1, and induce CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A in vitro. Midostaurin inhibits organic anion transporter polypeptide 1A1 and induces multidrug resistance protein 2 in vitro.

L904: ## 5 Sources of Clinical Data and Review Strategy
L905: ## 5.1. Table of Clinical Studies
L906: The Applicant submitted clinical study reports from 24 clinical trials of midostaurin (Table 2). Of these, 3 were studies of midostaurin in combination with intensive chemotherapy for treatment-naïve AML, 3 were studies of midostaurin monotherapy in previously untreated or relapsed or refractory AML or myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS), 5 were studies of midostaurin monotherapy in other types of cancer, and 13 were studies performed in healthy volunteers or patients without cancer. Raw datasets for clinical review were submitted for 5 of the 6 studies conducted in patients with AML; datasets were not available for the investigator-sponsored trial ADE02T. Raw datasets for clinical review were submitted for most of the 18 remaining studies; the exceptions were a old study for which datasets were not available (001) and Study A2120, which was terminated after enrollment of a single patient. See Table 2 for further details.

L931: ## 5.2. Review Strategy
L932: The key materials used for the review of efficacy and safety include:
L933: - NDA 207997
- Relevant published literature
- Relevant information in the public domain
L934: The review of efficacy was primarily based on analysis of Study A2301 and supported as follows:
L935: - Efficacy data from Study A2106 was used to support the analysis of dose selection
- Summarized efficacy data from investigator-sponsored Study ADE02T was used to support the analysis of the patient population covered by the proposed indication
- Efficacy data from Studies A2104, A2104E1 and A2104E2 were used to justify a limitation of use
L936: Results from all 24 trials listed in Table 2 were used to support the analysis of safety. Review emphasis was placed on safety data from Study A2301, but this was supported as follows:
L937: - Summarized safety data from Study A2106 and from investigator-sponsored Study ADE02T were used to support the analysis of the patient population covered by the proposed indication, and to support the analysis of dose selection in the indicated patient population
- Pooled safety data from all trials of midostaurin monotherapy in patients with cancer was used to analyze the impact of dose on safety, and to look for potential safety signals
- Pooled safety data from all trials of midostaurin monotherapy in healthy volunteers and patients without cancer was used look for potential safety signals
L938: All major efficacy and safety analyses were reproduced or audited. Summaries of data and statistical analyses by the reviewer were performed using JMP 12.0 (SAS Institute, Inc., Cary, NC), and Excel 2010 (Microsoft, Redmond, WA). MedDRA Adverse Events Diagnostic 1.3 (MAED) (FDA, Silver Spring, MD) was used to look for safety signals. All hazard ratios and p-values included in the time-to-event analyses in this review were calculated using an unstratified Cox proportional hazards method except where indicated. P-values were not adjusted for multiplicity except where indicated. See the statistician's review for additional analyses of efficacy endpoints.
L939: ## 6 Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L940: ## 6.1. Study A2301
L941: ## 6.1.1. Study Design
L942: ## Clinical Review
L943: Ashley Ward, MD
L944: NDA 207997
L945: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L946: ## Overview and Objective
L947: Study A2301 is a phase 3, randomized, double-blinded, placebo-controlled trial of midostaurin in combination with standard chemotherapy for the treatment of patients &lt; 60 years of age with newly diagnosed AML harboring an internal tandem duplication (ITD) or tyrosine kinase domain mutation (TKD) of FLT3. The primary objective of the study was to determine if the addition of midostaurin to daunorubicin/cytarabine induction, high-dose cytarabine consolidation, and continuation therapy improves OS in FLT3-ITD and FLT3-TKD patients with AML.
L948: The key secondary objective was to compare the EFS between the two treatment groups. Other secondary efficacy objectives were to compare the OS when patients who receive a hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HCST) are censored at the time of transplantation, the CR rate, the disease-free survival (DFS), the DFS rate at one year after completion of continuation therapy, and the HSCT rate between the two groups. Other secondary objectives were to assess the interaction between treatment outcome and pretreatment patient or disease characteristics, the toxicity of the experimental combination and the population pharmacokinetics of midostaurin and its two major metabolites.
L949: ## Trial Design
L950: Patients age 18 to 59 with an unequivocal diagnosis of AML (&gt;20% blasts in the bone marrow) and documented FLT3 mutation as determined by central laboratory testing who had not received prior therapy were randomized 1:1 to receive up to six 28-day cycles of standard chemotherapy in combination with either placebo or midostaurin (Figure 1) followed by up to twelve 28-day cycles of single agent maintenance therapy. Randomization was stratified by FLT3 mutation status (ITD allelic ratio &lt; 0.7, ITD allelic ratio ≥ 0.7, or TKD). Patients with M3 AML (APL), therapy-related AML, or central nervous system (CNS) leukemia were not eligible for the study.
L951: **Figure 1: Study A2301 – Study Design Schema**
L952: **Reviewer comment:** Lack of re-randomization prior to maintenance makes it difficult to make inferences about the effectiveness of maintenance therapy.
L953: ## Clinical Review
L954: Ashley Ward, MD
L955: NDA 207997
L956: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L957: A bone marrow aspiration was performed on day 21 of the first cycle of induction therapy. Patients without a CR were administered a second cycle of induction therapy beginning on or shortly after day 24, and patients with residual AML by bone marrow aspirate after completion of a second remission induction were removed from protocol therapy. Patients who achieved a CR after 1 or 2 cycles of induction therapy proceeded to receive 4 cycles of consolidation therapy, with each cycle beginning after recovery of absolute neutrophil count (ANC) to \ge 1000/\mu\text{L} and platelets \ge 100,000/\mu\text{L}. Patients who remained in CR by bone marrow aspirate after completion of consolidation therapy proceeded to maintenance therapy. Patients who were unable to complete four cycles of consolidation therapy due to toxicity but who remained in CR were allowed to proceed to maintenance therapy with approval from the study chair. Bone marrow aspirates to assess disease status were performed every four months during maintenance therapy. Patients whose AML relapsed at any time were removed from study therapy. All patients who enrolled on the study were followed for the primary endpoint of overall survival. There was no re-randomization prior to maintenance therapy, and cross-over was not allowed. Patients were allowed to proceed to HSCT at any time; these patients came off protocol-directed therapy but were followed for OS.
L958: ## *Eligibility criteria (summarized):*
L959: - Unequivocal diagnosis of AML (&gt;20\% blasts in the bone marrow based on the WHO classification), excluding M3 (acute promyelocytic leukemia). Patients with neurologic symptoms suggestive of CNS leukemia were recommended to have a lumbar puncture. Patients whose cerebrospinal fluid is positive for AML blasts were not eligible.
- Documented FLT3 mutation (ITD or point mutation) determined by analysis in a protocol-designated FLT3 screening laboratory.
- Age \ge 18 and &lt; 60 years.
- No prior chemotherapy for leukemia or MDS except: emergency leukapheresis, emergency treatment for hyperleukocytosis with hydroxyurea for \le 5 days, cranial radiotherapy for CNS leukostasis (one dose only) and growth factor / cytokine support.
- AML patients with a history of antecedent MDS were eligible, but must not have had prior cytotoxic therapy (e.g. azacitidine or decitabine).
- Patients who have developed therapy related AML after prior radiation therapy or chemotherapy for another cancer or disorder were not eligible.
- Patients with symptomatic congestive heart failure were not eligible.
- Bilirubin &lt; 2.5\times the upper limit of normal.
- Not pregnant and not nursing, and willing to use highly effective method of birth control.
L960: ## *Study assessments:*
L961: Bone marrow and peripheral blood samples for FLT3 mutation screening were obtained during the screening period and, in consenting patients, also at the time of relapse. Samples were
L962: ## Clinical Review
L963: Ashley Ward, MD
L964: NDA 207997
L965: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L966: shipped via overnight carrier on the day of collection to a central laboratory site for analysis.
L967: Bone marrow aspirate smears and biopsy specimens for central morphology review were obtained during screening, on day 21 of remission induction therapy and at additional time points as described above. Samples were shipped within one week of collection to a central laboratory site for analysis. Local biopsy and immunophenotyping reports, including differential cell counts, cytochemistry results and French-American-British (FAB) classification were collected along with the samples. If the day 21 bone marrow aspirate revealed &lt; 5% blasts in a cellular marrow (&gt;20%), then a second bone marrow aspiration was performed one week after recovery of ANC \ge 1000/\mu\text{L} and platelets \ge 100,000/\mu\text{L} to document complete response. The bone marrow examination to document complete response must be performed on or before day 60, regardless of whether the patient received 1 or 2 cycles of induction therapy.
L968: History and physical exam, including height, weight, body surface area, performance status, and drug toxicity assessment were collected at the time of screening (within 16 days prior to randomization). Screening laboratory assessments consisted of complete blood counts with differential (CBC), a comprehensive metabolic panel, coagulation studies, urinalysis, thyroid function tests and urine or serum pregnancy test in women of childbearing potential. Other screening assessments were an assessment of left ventricular ejection fraction by multigated (MUGA) scan or echocardiogram, a chest x-ray, and a lumbar puncture for patients with neurologic symptoms suggestive of CNS leukemia.
L969: History, physical exam, and drug toxicity assessment were repeated at least twice weekly during induction therapy, once weekly during consolidation therapy, and monthly during maintenance therapy. A CBC, basic metabolic panel, and tests of liver function were repeated at least weekly during induction and consolidation therapy, and monthly during maintenance therapy. Thyroid function tests were repeated at 1, 6 and 12 months after registration, and 30 days after discontinuation of study therapy. Electrocardiograms (ECGs) were collected for assessment of the QTc interval prior to midostaurin/placebo dose on days 8, 10, and 21 during each cycle of induction and consolidation, and on day 1 of each cycle of maintenance therapy.
L970: Peripheral blood was collected for pharmacokinetic (PK) assessments in consenting patients at the following time points:
L971: - Cycle 1 of remission induction therapy (days 8 and 21)
- Cycle 1 of consolidation therapy (days 8 and 21)
- Cycle 4 of consolidation therapy (days 8 and 21)
- Cycle 8 of maintenance therapy (day 1)
- Cycle 12 of maintenance therapy (day 1)
L972: All samples were to be collected prior to the morning midostaurin/placebo dose. Midostaurin and its major metabolites (CGP52421 and CGP62221) were analyzed at Novartis Pharma SAS, Bioanalytics, in France.
L973: ## Clinical Review
L974: Ashley Ward, MD
L975: NDA 207997
L976: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L977: ## *Dose modifications and management of toxicity:*
L978: Midostaurin/placebo was taken at approximately the same time each day with approximately 12 hours between the morning and evening doses. Each daily dose was taken with food and a glass of water (~240 mL). Patients were instructed to swallow capsules whole without chewing, and to not re-dose in the event of vomiting. Missed doses of midostaurin/placebo were not made up. Modifications of the dose and schedule of midostaurin/placebo were not allowed except in the following circumstances:
L979: - For QTc interval &gt; 470 ms and ≤ 500 ms, midostaurin/placebo dose was decreased to 50 mg once daily for the remainder of the cycle, then resumed at the initial dose in the next cycle if the QTc is ≤ 470 ms. Otherwise, midostaurin/placebo was continued at 50 mg once daily.
- For QTc interval &gt; 500 ms, midostaurin/placebo was held for the remainder of the cycle and resumed at the initial dose in the next cycle in which the QTc is ≤ 470 ms.
- Dose modifications for grade 1-2 non-hematologic toxicities were not allowed during induction and consolidation therapy. Drug holidays of up to 28 consecutive days for persistent grade 1-2 non-hematologic toxicities were allowed during maintenance.
- For grade 3-4 non-hematologic toxicities considered to be at least possibly related to midostaurin/placebo, midostaurin/placebo was interrupted and then resumed at the same dose upon resolution of the toxicity to grade ≤ 1 (in induction and consolidation) or ≤ grade 2 (in maintenance). If midostaurin/placebo was held for &gt;28 days for toxicity during maintenance therapy, patients were required to discontinue study therapy.
- For grade ≥ 3 pulmonary infiltrates, midostaurin/placebo was held for the remainder of the cycle and resumed (in a subsequent cycle) at the same dose when infiltrate resolved to grade ≤ 1.
- (Added in a protocol amendment) For grade 4 neutropenia during maintenance, midostaurin/placebo was held until ANC ≥ 100/µL, then resumed at previous dose. If neutropenia persisted longer than 2 weeks, patients were required to discontinue midostaurin/placebo.
L980: Initial and subsequent doses of daunorubicin were modified for hepatotoxicity as follows:
L981: - Total bilirubin ≤ 2 mg/dL: give 100% of daunorubicin dose
- Total bilirubin &gt; 2 to ≤ 3 mg/dL: give 75% of daunorubicin dose
- Total bilirubin &gt; 3 mg/dL: give 50% of daunorubicin dose
L982: For neurotoxicity grade ≥ 2 due to high dose cytarabine during consolidation therapy, cytarabine was discontinued for the remainder of the cycle. If the neurotoxicity resolved to grade ≤ 1, cytarabine could be resumed for the next consolidation therapy cycle with a dose modification from 3 g/m2 to 2 g/m2.
L983: All drug doses were based on actual body weight, with no modifications for obesity. Doses of
L984: ## Clinical Review
L985: Ashley Ward, MD
L986: NDA 207997
L987: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L988: chemotherapy were not adjusted for body weight changes in subsequent cycles unless the body weight change was at least 10%. Total administered dose of chemotherapy could be rounded up or down within a range of 5% of the actual calculated dose.
L989: ## *Concomitant medications:*
L990: Supportive care medications were allowed, including transfusions of blood and blood products, antibiotics, antiemetics and allopurinol. However, the use of aprepitant was not permitted. Treatment with hormones or other chemotherapeutic agents was not permitted except for steroids given for adrenal failure and hormones given for non-disease-related conditions (e.g. insulin for diabetes). Steroids were permitted for the treatment or prevention of hypersensitivity reactions or transfusion reactions. Brief use of corticosteroids as anti-emetics was permitted but not recommended. Myeloid growth factors were permitted only for neutropenic patients with prognostic factors predictive of clinical deterioration (e.g., pneumonia, hypotension, sepsis, fungal infection) as per American Society of Clinical Oncology (ASCO) guidelines.
L991: Physicians were advised that midostaurin is a competitive inhibitor of CYP3A4/5 and thus co-administration of drugs which are potent inducers (e.g., phenytoin) or, especially, potent inhibitors (e.g., itraconazole) of this isoenzyme could decrease or increase midostaurin concentrations, and, potentially, decrease the effectiveness or increase toxicity, respectively. However, such agents were not prohibited on this study.
L992: ## *Response criteria:*
L993: Responses must have been maintained with no evidence of AML for more than 4 weeks. Response assessments required a bone marrow aspirate, or, in the event of a dry tap, a bone marrow biopsy. Central histologic review was required for documentation of complete response following induction therapy. Response criteria were defined in the protocol and were based on the revised recommendations of the International Working Group in AML (Cheson et al, JCO 2003)
L994: Complete response / remission (CR) was defined as all of the following:
L995: - Peripheral blood: ANC \ge 1,000/\muL, platelet count \ge 100,000/\muL, no leukemic blasts in the peripheral blood and adequate erythroid recovery so that RBC transfusions are not necessary [time frame not defined]
- Bone marrow: adequate cellularity [not further defined], no Auer rods, and &lt; 5% blasts
- No extramedullary leukemia
L996: Complete response with incomplete platelet recovery (CRp) was defined as: all CR criteria are met except that platelets are &lt; 100,000/\muL.
L997: Partial remission (PR) was defined as: all CR criteria are met except that the bone marrow may
L998: ## Clinical Review
L999: Ashley Ward, MD
L1000: NDA 207997
L1001: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1002: contain 5-24% blasts.
L1003: Treatment failure was defined as: failure to achieve a CR by 60 days after the start of initial induction therapy. Patients who were treated appropriately, survived for &gt; 30 days after the start of study therapy, and failed to achieve remission due to persistent AML as determined by peripheral blood and bone marrow aspirate and biopsy were deemed to have treatment failure due to resistant disease. Patients who died within 30 days of starting protocol therapy were deemed to have treatment failure due to death of the patient.
L1004: **Reviewer comment:** It is unusual to require that a CR occur within 60 days after the first dose of therapy. The more widely accepted definition of primary refractory AML is failure to achieve a CR after two cycles of induction therapy, regardless of the timing of count recovery (Ravandi, Br J Haematol 2011).
L1005: Relapse was defined as any of the following, occurring after either CR, CRp, or PR:
L1006: - The reappearance of circulating blast cells not attributable to “overshoot” following recovery from myelosuppressive therapy
- &gt; 5% blasts in the marrow, not attributable to another cause (e.g., CSF, bone marrow regeneration)
- Development of extramedullary leukemia
L1007: ## Study Endpoints
L1008: The primary endpoint was overall survival, defined as the period from the date of randomization until death by any cause. Patients alive at the end of the study period were censored for this endpoint.
L1009: The key secondary endpoint (made key in Amendment 4) was EFS, defined as the time from randomization until an EFS event was observed. EFS events were defined as a failure to obtain a CR within 60 days after the first dose of protocol therapy (i.e., treatment failure), or relapse, or death from any cause. Patients who were alive and event-free at the end of the study period were censored for this endpoint.
L1010: Other secondary endpoints were:
L1011: - OS, where patients who received a HSCT are censored at the time of transplantation (added in Amendment 4)
- CR rate in the remission induction stage of the study
- DFS
- DFS rate one year after completing the planned continuation phase
- Rate of HSCT (added in Amendment 10)
L1012: DFS was defined only for patients who achieved a CR within 60 days after the first dose of study
L1013: ## Clinical Review
L1014: Ashley Ward, MD
L1015: NDA 207997
L1016: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1017: therapy, and was defined as the time from the date of first CR to relapse or death from any cause, whichever occurs first. Patients who were alive and disease-free at the end of the study period were censored for this endpoint.
L1018: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L1019: The baseline assumptions were as follows:
L1020: - The study would accrue 300 FLT3 mutation-positive patients per year
- 1 in 7 accrued patients would have FLT3-TKD and the other 6 would have FLT3-ITD
- Median OS is 15 months in the placebo arm and 21 months in the midostaurin arm
- CR rate is 75% in the placebo arm and 83% in the midostaurin arm
- DFS is 11 months in the placebo arm and 15.4 months in the midostaurin arm
- Early death rate (before CR is determined) is 7%
- Stem cell transplantation rate is 15%
L1021: Based on these assumptions, accrual of 514 patients over 1.7 years with a 2-year follow up would, in all cases with a Type I error of 0.025 (one-sided):
L1022: - Yield 374 OS events and provide 90% power to detect an OS hazard ratio of 0.714
- Provide 56% power to detect an 8% difference in CR rates between the two regimens
- Yield 433 EFS events and provide 95% power to detect a difference in median EFS
- Yield 335 DFS events and provide 87% power to detect a difference in median DFS
L1023: One interim analysis for the overall survival endpoint was planned, after 50% of expected events (187), with an alpha spend of 0.005. A conditional probability of rejecting the null hypothesis of &lt; 0.10 was planned to be grounds for stopping for early futility. This interim analysis occurred in June 2012 and the Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) concluded that the study should proceed without changes.
L1024: The original statistical analysis plan was finalized in June of 2011 based on protocol amendment 4, which increased the sample size to 714 patients and number of required OS events to 509 due to revised assumptions about the frequency of HSCT and FLT3-TKD on the study (see below). A revised statistical analysis plan was generated in 2015 due to slow accrual of OS events (see below), and was finalized prior to the primary analysis of the primary endpoint in July of 2015. The data cut-off date used for preparation of this marketing application was April 1, 2015.
L1025: The final analysis of the primary endpoint of OS was not event-driven, and was performed prior to reaching the planned 509 OS events. All deaths up to and including April 1, 2015, were included as OS events. Information regarding OS events obtained during a data sweep after the April 1, 2015 were used to “back-track” and determine the vital status on April 1, 2015. Patients with no post-baseline data were censored on the date of randomization. Patients known to be
L1026: ## Clinical Review
L1027: Ashley Ward, MD
L1028: NDA 207997
L1029: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1030: alive at their last contact were censored at their date of last contact before April 1, 2015. If date of last contact was missing from the follow-up case report form (CRF), it was imputed as the end date of the corresponding reporting period. All others were considered alive on April 1, 2015 and were censored for OS analysis on this date. If a death was reported without a corresponding death date, the missing date was imputed as the start date of the reporting period in the CRF in which the death was reported.
L1031: ## **Reviewer comment: Imputation of the date of last contact as the end date of the corresponding reporting period may artificially increase estimates of OS on both arms.**
L1032: For assessments of response/relapse, only data from CRFs collected prior to April 1, 2015, were used. CRFs collected after April 1, 2015, were not used to “back track” and determine clinical status on April 1, 2015. All responses were investigator-determined. A CR was only considered if it occurred within 60 days after the first administration of any study treatment. Treatment failure was defined as a failure to achieve a CR within 60 days after the first administration of any study treatment, and was an event considered for the calculation of EFS. The date of treatment failure was calculated by Novartis from the dates reported by the investigator as follows:
L1033: - For patients with a clinical assessment but who did not achieve a CR within 60 days after first administration of study drug, treatment failure date was the date of the last clinical assessment within the 60 day window
- For patients who had no clinical assessment and died within the 60 day window, treatment failure date was the date of death
- For patients who had no clinical assessment, did not die, and discontinued treatment within the 60 day window, treatment failure date was the date of last treatment administration
- For patients who had no clinical assessment, did not die, and did not discontinue treatment within the time window, treatment failure date was the randomization date
L1034: Relapses were events considered for the time-to-event endpoints EFS, DFS and remission duration. A relapse was only considered for patients who achieved a CR within 60 days after the first administration of any study treatment, and was calculated from the dates reported by the investigator as the earliest known date of relapse, even if observed after missing assessments.
L1035: The date of the last clinical assessment in response up to and including April 1, 2015, was used as the censoring date for the time-to-event endpoints EFS, DFS and remission duration. It was taken for patients who achieved CR and had no relapse up to and including the last clinical assessment.
L1036: The final analysis of the key secondary endpoint of EFS was not event-driven. A total of 717 patients accrued uniformly to the trial over 40 months. Based on an assumed median EFS of 8.5
L1037: ## Clinical Review
L1038: Ashley Ward, MD
L1039: NDA 207997
L1040: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1041: months for the placebo patients and 14 months for the midostaurin patients, 507 EFS events could have been observed with a minimum follow-up of 3.5 years for all patients. Under these assumptions, the trial would have a power of approximately 89% using a one-sided alpha of 2.5% to detect a hazard ratio of 1.34 using a Cox regression model.
L1042: For all time-to-event endpoints, a stratified log-rank test, adjusting for the FLT3 mutation strata used in the randomization, was used to test the null hypotheses and calculate the one-sided p-value. Stratified Cox regression models were used to provide estimates of the hazard ratios and associated 95% confidence intervals. Kaplan-Meier plots were used to depict events over time in each treatment arm.
L1043: The patient sets considered for the evaluation of the study were as follows:
L1044: - Full Analysis Set (FAS) was defined according to intent-to-treat (ITT) principles and included all randomized patients with a signed informed consent. Patients were analyzed according to treatment arm and stratum assigned at randomization. The FAS was used for the analyses of efficacy endpoints and baseline characteristics.
- The Per Protocol Set (PPS) was the subset of the FAS who received at least one dose of midostaurin/placebo and had no major protocol deviations that could affect response to treatment. The PPS was used for additional analyses of OS and EFS.
- The Safety Set included all patients with a signed informed consent that received at least one dose of midostaurin/placebo. Patients who were randomized to one arm but only received the study drug (midostaurin/placebo) of the other arm during the study were analyzed in the arm of the study drug they received. All patients who received at least one dose of the study drug they were randomized to were analyzed in their randomized treatment arm. The Safety Set was used for the analyses of safety endpoints, concomitant medications and treatment exposure.
- The Pharmacokinetic Set (PK Set) included all patients with a signed informed consent who received at least one dose of midostaurin and who provided at least one evaluable PK concentration. The PK Set was used for the analyses of PK endpoints.
L1045: ## Protocol Amendments
L1046: The study was activated on April 1, 2008, and the first patient was randomized to the study on July 3, 2008. Enrollment was completed in October of 2011, with 719 patients randomized. The study was revised a total of 10 times between activation and finalization in June of 2015. Key protocol revisions are summarized as follows:
L1047: - Amendment 2 (effective April 1, 2009, 101 patients enrolled) changed the dosing of midostaurin/placebo in maintenance from 14 days out of every 28 to continuous daily dosing in order to maintain midostaurin drug concentrations above the IC50.
- Amendment 3 (effective December 1, 2009, 279 patients enrolled) added the collection
L1048: ## Clinical Review
L1049: Ashley Ward, MD
L1050: NDA 207997
L1051: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1052: of concomitant medications to study procedures. Concomitant medications were collected retrospectively in Non-North-American sites, but not for the 68 North American patients that enrolled prior to the amendment.
L1053: - Amendment 4 (effective December 15, 2010, 549 patients enrolled) increased the sample size and the number of required OS events for the final analysis to restore study power. The revised study design required 714 patients, and the final analysis was planned when 509 OS events occurred, with 84% power to detect a hazard ratio of 0.78 with a Type I error of 0.025 (one-sided). These revisions were made due to interim review of blinded data that revealed that the rates of HSCT (25%) and FLT3-TKD mutations (26%) were higher than originally predicted. The study also added OS with censoring at the time of HSCT as a new secondary objective.
- Amendment 10 (effective June 15, 2015, 719 patients enrolled) changed the study design to perform the final analysis with a data cut-off of April 1, 2015, without waiting to achieve the required 509 events. At the same time, the secondary endpoint EFS was promoted to a key secondary endpoint to be tested in a hierarchical manner if the OS endpoint was significant, and HSCT rate was added as a secondary endpoint. These revisions were made due to slow accrual of OS events.
L1054: No major changes were made to the eligibility criteria or response criteria over the course of the study.
L1055: ## Data Quality and Integrity: Sponsor's Assurance
L1056: Alliance is the owner of [REDACTED] (b) (4) the protocol, the study database, and the data for this study. The study used study-specific paper case report forms (CRFs). All completed CRFs and corresponding source documents required by the protocol (i.e. pathology reports) were submitted by the sites to the Alliance Statistics and Data Center where the data was reviewed, entered into a database, and queried. In North American (NA) trial sites, where the study was sponsored by Alliance, Novartis and/or a designated contract research organization (CRO) appointed by Novartis performed quality control visits approximately twice yearly. Alliance performed off-site monitoring by checking sources sent by sites to confirm response and relapse. In NA sites, only portions of data were source-verified, and in only 224 of the 236 (94%) of the patients. Non-North American (NNA) trial sites were monitored by Novartis at frequencies varying from every 4 weeks to yearly, which included 100% source data verification of key data and 10% for non-key data for all subjects (n=481).
L1057: Data management followed Alliance Legacy Policies and Procedures. Novartis collaborated with the Alliance Statistical Data Center in the review of the blinded safety data via periodic transfers provided by Alliance to Novartis. All efficacy data were cleaned by Novartis and the primary efficacy analyses were prepared by Alliance statisticians for the DSMB meeting held in July 2015. Following DSMB review, the data was further cleaned, validated and transferred to
L1058: Novartis in preparation for regulatory submission. After Novartis locked the database, the treatment codes were transferred from Alliance to Novartis to allow the analysis to begin.
L1059: As the Applicant for the midostaurin marketing authorization, Novartis stated that they have performed due diligence to ensure the quality and integrity of the data from this collaborative group study. Novartis also states that the database for Study A2301 is complete and accurate for the benefit-risk assessment of midostaurin in this study.
L1060: ## 6.1.2. Study Results
L1061: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1062: The applicant provided attestation that this study was conducted in accordance with U.S. regulations governing the protection of human subjects, institutional review boards, and the obligations of clinical investigators in accordance with good clinical practice (GCP).
L1063: ## Financial Disclosure
L1064: A summary of financial disclosures for Study A2301 is provided in the appendix (Section 13.2). The applicant submitted financial disclosure information from 95.3% of investigators at sites in the United States and 99.9% of investigators at all other sites. All missing financial disclosure information was from sub-investigators. Two primary investigators and 3 sub-investigators had financial disclosures. Four of the disclosures, which included both primary investigators, were for grants in excess of $25,000 from the study sponsor to the investigator to fund ongoing research. These sites enrolled a total of [b] patients. The fifth disclosure was for a proprietary or financial interest in the product tested in the clinical study of undisclosed amount. This site enrolled [b] patients, but analysis of the OS data from the subset of patients enrolled at this site revealed a treatment effect favoring the placebo arm. Removal of all [b] patients enrolled at these sites from the primary analysis did not substantially affect the result. Thus, enrollment of patients by these investigators does not appear to have biased the outcome of the study.
L1065: ## Patient Disposition
L1066: Of 3279 patients screened, 719 patients were randomized in Study A2301. Two patients were excluded due to problems with their informed consent, resulting in 717 patients included in the FAS. Of these, 360 were randomized to the midostaurin arm and 357 to the placebo arm. The number of patients that received any portion of study therapy in each treatment phase and the reasons for treatment discontinuation are shown in Table 3. Patients were designated as having received treatment in a study cycle if they received at least one dose of protocol-designated therapy in that cycle. Patients were determined to have completed all 12 cycles of maintenance if at least 12 cycles of maintenance therapy were reported and the investigator checked the box “completed treatment per protocol criteria” in the CRF.

L1075: Only 17% of all study participants completed all protocol-designated therapy, including 12 cycles of maintenance therapy. Nearly one third of the subjects on both arms discontinued study treatment to pursue alternative therapy, most frequently HSCT. More patients in the placebo arm required a second induction (28% vs 23%). Discontinuations for relapsed or refractory disease occurred more frequently on the placebo arm (29% vs 24%), while discontinuations for adverse events occurred more frequently on the midostaurin arm (10% vs 6%).
L1076: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L1077: A total of 1,743 protocol deviations were reported for 493 patients on Study A2301. The majority (1,626 deviations, 93%) were considered by the applicant to be minor deviations. As deviations were reported in narrow categories (e.g., missing bone marrow assessment during continuation), the FDA summarized the minor deviations into broader categories (e.g., missing bone marrow assessment) for analysis. Summarized, the most frequent minor deviations were
L1078: ## Clinical Review
L1079: Ashley Ward, MD
L1080: NDA 207997
L1081: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1082: treatment errors (580 deviations affecting 262 patients), missing bone marrow assessments (453 deviations affecting 229 patients), and missing laboratory assessments (400 deviations affecting 36 patients).
L1083: Major protocol deviations occurred in 15% of the study patients, with similar frequency on both treatment arms (Table 4). A total of 43 patients (6%) failed to meet one or more eligibility criteria that had the potential to influence evaluation of the primary endpoint; the majority because they received prior treatment for leukemia or MDS. However, of the 34 patients who received prohibited prior treatment for leukemia or MDS, 32 of them had received hydroxyurea in excess of 5 days as their only prohibited prior therapy, which is unlikely to have substantially influenced the results of the study. Seventy patients (10%) had one or more major dosing violations involving the study drug; the majority because they did not receive any midostaurin/placebo. As none of the major deviations were likely to bias the study in favor of the midostaurin arm, all patients, including those with major protocol deviations were included in the FDA's analysis of efficacy endpoints.

L1111: ## Clinical Review
L1112: Ashley Ward, MD
L1113: NDA 207997
L1114: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1115: FLT3 status was confirmed by a central laboratory. However, there are multiple central laboratory FLT3 results provided for 49 of the 717 patients (7%). The study database provided by the applicant contains the date the sample was received by the central lab, but not the collection date, and therefore provides insufficient information to determine which of the samples were obtained at baseline. In a response (dated January 31, 2017, SDN 73) to an information request by the FDA, the applicant stated that they assumed that the sample with the earliest receipt date was the baseline sample. For patients with more than one result provided with the same receipt date (in all such cases, one result indicates FLT3-TKD and a second indicates FLT3-ITD), the applicant classified the patient as FLT3-ITD. For the purposes of this review, the FDA took the same approach, although the results must be interpreted with caution.
L1116: Although 20 patients (7 on the midostaurin arm and 13 on the placebo arm) were incorrectly stratified for randomization based on their FLT3 mutation status, FLT3 mutation status was balanced across treatment arms. The incidence of FLT3-TKD mutations in the general untreated AML population is approximately 5% (Bacher et al, Blood 2008), while the incidence of FLT3-ITD mutations in the same population is 23-27% depending on age (Levis, Hematology ASH Educ Program, 2013). The observed ratio of patients with FLT3-TKD to those with FLT3-ITD observed on Study A2301 was thus somewhat higher than has been observed in the general population. Otherwise, the disease characteristics at baseline observed on Study A2301 generally reflect the general population of patients with previously untreated AML.
L1117: Nucleophosmin (NPM1) mutation analysis was performed on a subset of patients enrolled on Study A2301 (n=563) as part of a separate companion protocol. This data was requested by the FDA during review of the application and is summarized in Table 6. NPM1 mutation results were available from 79% of the patients enrolled on Study A2301. NPM1 mutations were slightly more frequent on the placebo arm (60% vs 55%).
L1118: A limited amount of cytogenetic information was captured in the CRF by the investigator in the WHO classification and is summarized in Table 6; the majority of patients had a WHO classification of “Other”. The protocol specified central review of cytogenetics, but in their response (dated January 31, 2017) to an FDA information request, the applicant stated that this data was never entered into the Alliance database. Therefore, there is insufficient information available to analyze the cytogenetic/molecular prognostic category of patients enrolled on Study A2301.
L1119: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L1120: The applicant did not do an analysis of treatment compliance, and the datasets provided by the applicant were not sufficient to allow the FDA to independently analyze treatment compliance, as reasons for missed doses were not captured in early versions of the CRFs and reasons for
L1121: ## Clinical Review
L1122: Ashley Ward, MD
L1123: NDA 207997
L1124: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1125: missed doses other than for toxicity were not consistently captured by later versions of the CRFs. However, the median dose intensity of midostaurin/placebo was high (&gt;94%) on both treatment groups. Please see Section 8.2.1 for an analysis of exposure to the study drugs.
L1126: Concomitant medications were collected throughout the treatment period for all Non-North American (NNA) patients, but only after Amendment 4 for patients in North America (NA). The analysis of concomitant medications was thus performed only on the subset of patients (n=649, 91%) for whom medications were recorded from the time of randomization. Only the following were required to be reported per protocol: antibiotics, antivirals, antifungals, proton pump inhibitors, H2 antagonists, NSAIDs, opioids, antiemetics, antihistamines, corticosteroids, growth factors, diuretics, antihypertensives, and other CYP3A4 inhibitors or inducers.
L1127: The most frequently used (in &gt;40% of patients) concomitant medications in the induction phase were vancomycin, furosemide, acyclovir, ondansetron, acetaminophen and pantoprazole. The most frequently used (in &gt;40% of patients) concomitant medications in the consolidation phase were ondansetron, dexamethasone, levofloxacin, acetaminophen, pantoprazole, acyclovir, furosemide and vancomycin. The most frequently used (in &gt;10% of all patients) concomitant medications in the maintenance phase were pantoprazole, acyclovir and ibuprofen.
L1128: The use of concomitant CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers was of particular interest due to the pharmacokinetics of midostaurin (see Section 4.5 of this review for further details). Moderate or strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were used in 61%, 46% and 11% of patients in the induction, consolidation, and maintenance phases, respectively, and were balanced across treatment groups. The most frequently used moderate or strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were fluconazole, ciprofloxacin, posaconazole and voriconazole. Only 6 patients used a moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducer (nafcillin, phenytoin or carbamazepine) while on Study A2301.
L1129: Hematopoietic growth factor use during induction and consolidation therapy was generally balanced across both treatment arms. No growth factor use was reported for any patient during the maintenance phase of therapy. Filgrastim was used in 17% of patients on the midostaurin arm and 13% of patients on the placebo arm, while peg-filgrastim was used in 4% of patients on the midostaurin arm and 7% of patients on the placebo arm. Erythropoietin was used by only one patient, in the placebo group.
L1130: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L1131: The primary endpoint of Study A2301 was overall survival. The median time from the date of randomization to data cut-off date (April 1, 2015) was 60.2 months in both groups, with a minimum of 42 months in both groups. The FDA identified 357 death events, 171 (48%) on the midostaurin arm and 186 (52%) on the placebo arm. The remaining patients (n=360) were censored. The majority of the censored patients (292, 81%) had a date of last contact after the
L1132: ## Clinical Review
L1133: Ashley Ward, MD
L1134: NDA 207997
L1135: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1136: data cut-off date, and were thus censored on the data cut-off date. The other 68 patients (19%) had a date of last contact prior to the data cut-off date, and were thus censored on the date of last contact. Imputation of the date of last contact was required for only one patient (Subject ID 115957), and is thus unlikely to have influenced the outcome. Due to the interim analysis, the alpha available for the final analysis of the primary endpoint was 0.0478. There was a statistically significant difference (p=0.019) in OS that favored midostaurin treatment, with a hazard ratio of 0.77 (95% CI: 0.63, 0.96) (Figure 2).
L1137: **Figure 2: Study A2301 – Overall Survival**
L1138: Source: FDA analysis
L1139: Estimates of median OS are not informative since the curves for both treatment arms plateau after approximately 36 months. To get a more reliable estimate of the magnitude of the treatment benefit of midostaurin, when added to standard of care, the FDA analyzed restricted mean survival time, or the average survival up to the follow-up time, for both arms (Table 7).

L1152: Neither type of baseline FLT3 mutation nor presence of baseline NPM1 mutation appeared to substantially influence the treatment effect of midostaurin on OS. An analysis of OS by age group (arbitrarily selected as age above and below the median) suggests a trend for improved treatment benefit in younger patients. This reviewer believes that the observation that midostaurin did not appear to confer a survival advantage to females compared to placebo on Study A2301 is believed to be at least in part related to the high frequency of NPM1 mutations

L1181: ## Data Quality and Integrity – Reviewers’ Assessment
L1182: The quality of the original data submission was insufficient to evaluate and review the submission due to missing required information and poor organization. The applicant addressed the most significant of these issues prior to the filing date and thus the application was filed. However, residual issues with missing information limited some of the analyses, as described in this review. The FDA issued multiple requests for information throughout the review period, including a request for NPM1 mutation data from patients on Study A2301, which aided in the evaluation of efficacy. The clinical reviewer audited a sample of case report forms from Study A2301 for consistency with datasets and patient narratives; the quality and integrity of the data required to evaluate the primary endpoint was acceptable.
L1183: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L1184: The key secondary endpoint was EFS, defined as the time from randomization until an EFS event was observed. EFS events were defined as a failure to obtain a CR within 60 days after the first dose of protocol therapy, or relapse, or death from any cause. Patients who were alive and event-free at the end of the study period were censored at the date of last clinical assessment prior to the data cut-off date. See Table 10 for a summary of the FDA’s analysis of EFS.

L1190: ## Clinical Review
L1191: Ashley Ward, MD
L1192: NDA 207997
L1193: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1194: **Reviewer comment:** More patients censored for being in remission on the midostaurin arm (75, 73%) had a disease evaluation within 6 months of the study cut-off date than patients on the placebo arm (44, 57%). As patients without frequent disease evaluations could have early relapses that had not yet brought them to clinical attention, the higher number of patients in the placebo group without recent disease assessment could have biased the estimation of EFS effect size in favor of the placebo group.
L1195: The Applicant reported a total of 536 EFS events (256 on the midostaurin arm, 280 on the placebo arm), with a median EFS of 8.2 months for patients on the midostaurin arm versus 3.0 months for patients on the placebo arm (HR 0.78, one-sided p=0.002) (A2301 Clinical Study Report Section 11.3.1.3). However, the FDA observed that for patients who had no assessment or who did not achieve CR by day 60, the applicant assigned the EFS date as the date of last adequate response assessment prior to day 60. This method of assigning event dates allows for differences in EFS to result from event date assignment rather than from a treatment effect, which is undesirable.
L1196: The FDA analyzed EFS by instead assigning the date of treatment failure as day +1 for patients that did not have a CR documented by day +60 (Figure 5). This yields a median EFS of 8.3 months on the midostaurin arm versus 3.6 months on the placebo arm (HR 0.80, one-sided p=0.012). Of note, the hazard does not appear to be proportional over time, and most of the difference in the EFS curves between the two treatment arms appears to come from excess treatment failures on the placebo arm during induction therapy.
L1197: **Figure 5: Study A2301 – Event-Free Survival**
L1198: Source: FDA analysis
L1199: ## Clinical Review
L1200: Ashley Ward, MD
L1201: NDA 207997
L1202: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1203: Although the protocol defined CR as occurring within 60 days after starting study treatment, this definition is not standard. The FDA thus performed a sensitivity analysis on EFS, defining CR as occurring after 1 or 2 cycles of induction therapy. Sixty-one subjects (34 on the midostaurin arm and 27 on the placebo arm) who were considered treatment failures per protocol but who achieved CR after a median of 72 days (range 61 to 108 days) were recoded for this analysis. The analysis showed a continued benefit of midostaurin on EFS, with a hazard ratio of 0.74 (95% CI: 0.62, 0.87).
L1204: A summary of other secondary endpoints is provided in Table 11. Estimates of DFS and CR by day 60 were consistent with the primary and key secondary analyses. Transplantation rates were slightly higher on the midostaurin arm.

L1210: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1211: With the exception of dose reductions for prolongation of QT interval, and dose reductions for neutropenia during the maintenance phase (both of which occurred infrequently), dose modifications were not allowed on Study A2301. All patients received 50 mg bid on scheduled days, except during treatment interruptions. See the clinical pharmacology review for an evaluation of exposure-response.
L1212: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1213: Figure 3 suggested that midostaurin improved survival in males but not in females. To further explore this observation, the FDA performed two additional analyses. The first was an analysis of secondary endpoints by gender, to determine whether Study A2301 provides consistent
L1214: ## Clinical Review
L1215: Ashley Ward, MD
L1216: NDA 207997
L1217: Rydapt® (midostaurin)
L1218: evidence against the use of midostaurin in females across endpoints. The second was an analysis of NPM1 mutations by gender and treatment arm, since NPM1 mutations are a positive prognostic indicator that frequently co-occurs with FLT3 mutations, are more common in women, and could, in theory, be a predictor of response to midostaurin since midostaurin is a multi-kinase inhibitor with broad in vitro effects.
L1219: A summary of efficacy results from Study A2301 by gender is provided in Table 12. Women treated on the midostaurin arm appeared to have prolonged EFS, prolonged DFS, increased CR rates, and increased likelihood of HSCT by day 60 compared with women on the placebo arm. These secondary endpoints all point to consistent clinical activity of midostaurin in women. The markedly superior survival of females compared to males on the placebo arm is surprising, and likely accounts for at least some of the discrepancy in overall survival benefit by gender.
